# MDSE Rule-to-Model Traceability Matrix

**Audit date:** 2026-10-25. **Basis:** all 386 rule-reference IDs on `MDSE_v2_live/main`, with the current `MDSE_v2/governance/RULESET.md` text and the live decomposition, requirement, and verification registers.

**Critical distinction:** This is an inventory of existing provenance and *proposed* model connections. The registers do not establish validated native SysML requirement-to-function relationships. A candidate note is not an approved requirement; a verification definition is not a test result.

## Method and sources

- [Original source ruleset](https://github.com/spencerskelly/MDSE_v2/blob/main/governance/RULESET.md)
- [Rule reference register](70_Rule_References/RULE_TRACE_REGISTER.csv)
- [Analysis/decomposition register](71_Rule_Analysis/DECOMPOSITION_REGISTER.csv)
- [Requirement candidate register](72_Derived_Requirements/REQUIREMENT_CANDIDATES.csv)
- [Verification candidate register](73_Derived_Verification/VERIFICATION_CANDIDATES.csv)
- [GOV-001 owner decision / GOV-002 pending review](74_Semantic_Review/GOV_RULE_TRACE_REVIEW_2026-10-25.md)

**Row policy:** one row per derived requirement and one row per derived verification case. A rule without these gets one explicit `none` row. Nine additional atomic WKB-008 requirement notes are counted separately. Analysis and rule-reference Info notes are provenance, not additional model concepts.

**Columns:** `Original rule` reproduces the complete currently available normative line (may differ from older pinned reference revision); `Derived element` states each registered element and its recorded candidate statement; `Existing elements` gives proposed function, use case, and object IDs; `Stakeholder need` does not invent missing rule-to-need links; `Evidence status` identifies the unapproved or provisional trace.

| [GOV-011](70_Rule_References/GOV/GOV-001_020/GOV-011.md) — Proposed · Standing — One coordinated release manifest pins compatible Base Vault, Importer, Bootstrap, Workbench and SysML rule/schema versions and hashes. Base owns default assets and managed settings; Bootstrap owns installation and upgrades; Workbench owns interactive model writes; Importer owns import reconciliation. No tool overwrites another tool's user-owned data. Author identity is provenance, not authentication; credentials stay in OS or service stores, never shared YAML. (REG-040, V2-58 (Shared Note and Release Contract)) | **No derived requirement or verification definition** | Function: `BEH-00002`, `BEH-00007`; Use case: `UC-00001`, `UC-00008`; Object: `OBJ-00002`, `OBJ-00004` | No direct rule-to-need link in registers | Proposed; supporting; rule-derived link is provenance, other links provisional |
| [GOV-012](70_Rule_References/GOV/GOV-001_020/GOV-012.md) — Active · Standing — Tool ownership: Workbench owns UI behavior, disposable index, ModelRef navigation, schema-valid edit transactions and Review disposition (not semantics, importer mapping or release packaging). Bootstrap owns safe activation repair, release verification and author registration. Importer owns source translation, naming/identity allocation and import evidence (not editing or plugin management). Base builder owns the runtime payload, governed plugin config and pins/hashes. Cross-vault is deferred (archived resolver is reference only). A tool may not silently broaden or redefine model semantics; archived material never overrides a current source. | [MOD-075](70_Rule_References/MOD/MOD-061_080/MOD-075.md) — Active · Standing · live — For a SysML v2 relationship, legal endpoints and ownership come from the native SysML construct and its typed model, not from `relationships.yaml`; the authoritative `## SysML v2` block is validated independently. Endpoint restrictions (`from`, `to`, `sameClass` and exclusions) in `relationships.yaml` apply only to relationships explicitly approved as MDSE-only YAML links. Until OD-01 completes the field-by-field split, V1 entries in that schema do not authorize, prohibit or duplicate native SysML relationships. | [MOD-075](70_Rule_References/MOD/MOD-061_080/MOD-075.md) — Active · Standing · live — For a SysML v2 relationship, legal endpoints and ownership come from the native SysML construct and its typed model, not from `relationships.yaml`; the authoritative `## SysML v2` block is validated independently. Endpoint restrictions (`from`, `to`, `sameClass` and exclusions) in `relationships.yaml` apply only to relationships explicitly approved as MDSE-only YAML links. | [MOD-076](70_Rule_References/MOD/MOD-061_080/MOD-076.md) — Active · Standing · live — For native SysML v2 relationships, Workbench and importer read the forward meaning from the authoritative `## SysML v2` block and derive reverse navigation from it; no SysML-defined inverse is persisted in YAML. For a relationship approved as MDSE-only YAML under OD-01, any stored inverse is a non-authoritative derived field: the forward value wins on disagreement. The translator and Workbench, not Nodian, are the only writers of such MDSE-only inverses. This does not approve the still-legacy schema entries before OD-01 classification. | [MOD-165](70_Rule_References/MOD/MOD-161_180/MOD-165.md) — Active · P1 · live — The SysML v2 language is documented one note per construct (element and relationship) in `governance/language/`, built from `catalog.json`. Each note gives the description, the elements it can come from and go to, what separates it from the most similar construct, an example, and how often engineers use it (Everyday, Common, Occasional, Rare). The notes ship in every vault under `99_System/04_Language`. Entries are grouped as language constructs, standard-library concepts and modeling patterns, and each note states separately whether the construct is documented, recognized by the scanner, written by the importer, and accepted by a SysML v2 parser; a listed construct is not claimed as supported. | [IMP-096](70_Rule_References/IMP/IMP-081_100/IMP-096.md) — Active · P1 — Structure, links, quantities and multiplicity are defined by the SysML v2 block and are not repeated in `## Source: EA`. Relationships SysML v2 has no form for, and identifying properties, stay in the YAML (`describes`, `eaType`, `uid`, `id`). `## Source: EA` holds only what is stored nowhere else: the note's EA GUID, the original name when the file name differs, alias, the kept tags, trigger and guard text, unresolved connectors and attachments. EA author, created date, type, stereotype and package are not written: the uid carries date and author, `eaType` carries type and stereotype, the folder carries the package. (D-0027) *Note: Owner-approved 2026-10-19 (chat).* | [REQ-31161](72_Derived_Requirements/IMP/REQ-31161_31180/REQ-31161%20-%20IMP-096%20Statement%201.md) — **Draft requirement:** "Structure, links, quantities and multiplicity are defined by the SysML v2 block and are not repeated in `## Source: EA`." | Function: `BEH-00002`, `BEH-00004`, `BEH-00003`; Use case: `UC-00001`, `UC-00003`, `UC-00005`, `UC-00002`; Object: `OBJ-00001`, `OBJ-00002` | No direct rule-to-need link in registers | Active; direct; rule-derived link is provenance, other links provisional |

| [IMP-101](70_Rule_References/IMP/IMP-101_120/IMP-101.md) — Active · P1 — An EA Port becomes a port usage written `port name : [~]PortDef[mult]`. The type is the Port's PDATA1 (an EA interface Class, which is a port definition, IMP-104); the Classifier field is not the type. The multiplicity is the Multiplicity field, read as for parts; `~` is written when the object has an `isConjugated` tag with a true value (`true`, `yes`, `1` or EA's `-1`). A Port whose PDATA1 is not a port definition (a connector, a signal) is written without a type, the target is kept as a `- Port &lt;name&gt; is typed by [[…]]` line in Source, and the usage is flagged (`UNRESOLVED_PORT_TYPE:TARGET_IS_…`). (D-0033, D-0037) | [REQ-31173](72_Derived_Requirements/IMP/REQ-31161_31180/REQ-31173%20-%20IMP-101%20Statement%201.md) — **Draft requirement:** "An EA Port becomes a port usage written `port name : [~]PortDef[mult]`." | Function: `BEH-00002`, `BEH-00004`, `BEH-00003`; Use case: `UC-00001`, `UC-00003`, `UC-00005`, `UC-00002`; Object: `OBJ-00001`, `OBJ-00002` | No direct rule-to-need link in registers | Active; direct; rule-derived link is provenance, other links provisional |

| [IMP-102](70_Rule_References/IMP/IMP-101_120/IMP-102.md) — Active · P1 — A file or folder name is made safe for the browser's folder writer and for Windows: no control, bidi, line-separator or special characters, no lone surrogates, no trailing dot or space, and a device name (Con, Aux, Nul, Prn, Com0-9, Lpt0-9) is reserved with any extension, so `Con.pdf` becomes `Con_.pdf`. | [IMP-103](70_Rule_References/IMP/IMP-101_120/IMP-103.md) — Active · P1 — No folder or file name is cut to fit a path length; the 400-character path limit is handled after the import, not by the importer. A name is changed only for the safety rules of IMP-102 (the 150-character part limit, which is marked). The folder write retries a file up to six times with a growing delay on InvalidStateError, NoModificationAllowedError or NotReadableError (antivirus, cloud sync or the indexer touching a new file), and a write error names the browser error, its message and the path. (D-0036) | [REQ-31179](72_Derived_Requirements/IMP/REQ-31161_31180/REQ-31179%20-%20IMP-103%20Statement%201.md) — **Draft requirement:** "No folder or file name is cut to fit a path length; the 400-character path limit is handled after the import, not by the importer. | [IMP-106](70_Rule_References/IMP/IMP-101_120/IMP-106.md) — Active · P1 — A Port under a Part is the Part's block's own port and is not written as a usage of the Part. It merges, with one hop and never up the redefinition chain: (1) PDATA3 names a Port owned by the Part's block; (2) else the block has exactly one port with the same non-blank name; (3) else it is added to the block as a port of its own, flagged for review, and same-named ones on one block combine. A Part with no block defers the Port (`USAGE_WITHOUT_NOTE_OWNER`). A merged Port types an untyped block port from its PDATA1. (V1 W-114, W-130; D-0037) | [REQ-31184](72_Derived_Requirements/IMP/REQ-31181_31200/REQ-31184%20-%20IMP-106%20Statement%201.md) — **Draft requirement:** "A Port under a Part is the Part's block's own port and is not written as a usage of the Part." | Function: `BEH-00002`, `BEH-00007`, `BEH-00004`, `BEH-00003`; Use case: `UC-00001`, `UC-00008`, `UC-00009`, `UC-00003`, `UC-00005`, `UC-00002`; Object: `OBJ-00001`, `OBJ-00002` | No direct rule-to-need link in registers | Active; direct; rule-derived link is provenance, other links provisional |

| [IMP-113](70_Rule_References/IMP/IMP-101_120/IMP-113.md) — Active · P1 — An Aggregation or Composition makes the child a usage in the whole, of the kind its two definitions are: part/part is `part`, action/action `action`, state/state `state`, item/item `item`, requirement/requirement `requirement`, part/port def `port`, use case/use case `use case`. The whole is the end marked composite or shared. Every connector is its own usage, even with the same two ends; it takes the child end's role name, else the child's name with `_2` suffixes, and the child end's multiplicity (none is invented). An EA Part or nested element of that type is matched to one connector and not repeated. A connector whose whole and part sit in one cycle of ownership is left out as OWNERSHIP_CYCLE. A part that is the child of an interface is AGGREGATION_PART_IN_INTERFACE (review). (Test_Vault_ approved aggregation docs 2026-10-17; D-0038) | [REQ-31192](72_Derived_Requirements/IMP/REQ-31181_31200/REQ-31192%20-%20IMP-113%20Statement%201.md) — **Draft requirement:** "An Aggregation or Composition makes the child a usage in the whole, of the kind its two definitions are: part/part is `part`, action/action `action`, state/state `state`, item/item `item`, requirement/requirement `requirement`, part/port def `port`, use case/use case `use case`." | Function: `BEH-00002`, `BEH-00004`, `BEH-00003`; Use case: `UC-00001`, `UC-00003`, `UC-00005`, `UC-00002`; Object: `OBJ-00001`, `OBJ-00002` | No direct rule-to-need link in registers | Active; direct; rule-derived link is provenance, other links provisional |

| [IMP-115](70_Rule_References/IMP/IMP-101_120/IMP-115.md) — Active · P1 · live — When an EA relationship involves an MDSE-only note (Issue, Change, Info, Artifact, Decision), emit a supported descriptive YAML relation only when source meaning is established: `describes` for a describing Info/Artifact/Document, `affects` for an Issue with supported target semantics, `dependsOn` for compatible Dependency or Usage, and MDSE-only `hasChild` for an approved non-SysML note aggregation. Otherwise preserve each connector as a `flagLink` in the owning or holding note and a `deferred_review` ledger row, keeping connector GUID, family, source/target identities and reason `PROVISIONAL_LINK`. Do not create new `tracesTo` YAML links, assign a `native` disposition, or infer a SysML relationship. Pre-existing `tracesTo` values are historical input requiring explicit later reconciliation, not authority for new writes. Source: EA preserves a review line for deferred connectors; approved YAML relations are not repeated there. (REG-291, REG-293, W-288; D-0010, D-0038, D-0069, D-0070) | [REQ-31199](72_Derived_Requirements/IMP/REQ-31181_31200/REQ-31199%20-%20IMP-115%20Statement%201.md) — **Draft requirement:** "A relationship end that is an MDSE note (Issue, Change, Info, Artifact, Decision) has no SysML form and becomes a YAML link on the source note: `describes` when the note is Info, Artifact or Document, `dependsOn` for Dependency and Usage, `hasChild` for Aggregation, and otherwise the provisional `tracesTo` with PROVISIONAL_LINK." | Function: `BEH-00002`, `BEH-00004`; Use case: `UC-00001`, `UC-00003`, `UC-00005`; Object: `OBJ-00001`, `OBJ-00002` | No direct rule-to-need link in registers | Active; direct; rule-derived link is provenance, other links provisional |

| [IMP-116](70_Rule_References/IMP/IMP-101_120/IMP-116.md) — Active · P1 · live — A ControlFlow between action usages or a StateFlow between state usages in one known owning definition is a native SysML v2 succession or transition in that owner (IMP-098), retaining any explicit trigger, guard and effect. If endpoints do not share a valid known owner and no native relationship can be emitted without inventing ownership, preserve the connector GUID, source/target and original trigger/guard/effect as Source: EA evidence plus a `flagLink` and `deferred_review` ledger row with `FLOW_NO_COMMON_OWNER` or `FLOW_ENDPOINT_NOT_USAGE`; **do not write a YAML `precedes` edge** for temporal SysML semantics. Existing YAML `precedes` links are historical data pending OD-01 and are not bulk-rewritten. | [IMP-124](70_Rule_References/IMP/IMP-121_140/IMP-124.md) — Active · P1 — A usage or definition name keeps what makes the EA name different. A voltage puts its V at the decimal point and takes its sign as a letter: `+3.3VDC` is `P3V3DC`, `-13.3VDC` is `N13V3DC`, `12.5V` is `12V5`, `12VDC` stays `12VDC`; an unsigned voltage that starts the name takes `P`, because an identifier cannot start with a digit (`3.3VDC` is `P3V3DC`, so it and `+3.3VDC` meet and the second is told apart by its type). Elsewhere a leading `+` is `plus`, a leading `-` is `minus`, a point between digits is `p`, `&` is `And`, `%` is `Pct`, `#` is `No`. A name that is only a number is named after its type (`wire1`, not `_1`). Where the identifier is not just a respelling of the EA name, the EA name is recorded in `## Source: EA`. Two usages with one name take the name of their type to tell them apart (`a`, then `a_IConductor`); only a repeat of the same type takes `_2`. Quoted SysML names are not used, because every Workbench reader would need to change. Definitions keep the full EA title as their name. (D-0041, D-0042; amends IMP-095, IMP-112) | [REQ-31215](72_Derived_Requirements/IMP/REQ-31201_31220/REQ-31215%20-%20IMP-124%20Statement%201.md) — **Draft requirement:** "A usage or definition name keeps what makes the EA name different. A voltage puts its V at the decimal point and takes its sign as a letter: `+3.3VDC` is `P3V3DC`, `-13.3VDC` is `N13V3DC`, `12.5V` is `12V5`, `12VDC` stays `12VDC`; an unsigned voltage that starts the name takes `P`, because an identifier cannot start with a digit (`3.3VDC` is `P3V3DC`, so it and `+3.3VDC` meet and the second is told apart by its type)." | Function: `BEH-00002`, `BEH-00004`, `BEH-00003`; Use case: `UC-00001`, `UC-00003`, `UC-00005`, `UC-00002`; Object: `OBJ-00001`, `OBJ-00002` | No direct rule-to-need link in registers | Active; direct; rule-derived link is provenance, other links provisional |

A voltage puts its V at the decimal point and takes its sign as a letter: `+3.3VDC` is `P3V3DC`, `-13.3VDC` is `N13V3DC`, `12.5V` is `12V5`, `12VDC` stays `12VDC`; an unsigned voltage that starts the name takes `P`, because an identifier cannot start with a digit (`3.3VDC` is `P3V3DC`, so it and `+3.3VDC` meet and the second is told apart by its type). Elsewhere a leading `+` is `plus`, a leading `-` is `minus`, a point between digits is `p`, `&` is `And`, `%` is `Pct`, `#` is `No`. A name that is only a number is named after its type (`wire1`, not `_1`). Where the identifier is not just a respelling of the EA name, the EA name is recorded in `## Source: EA`. Two usages with one name take the name of their type to tell them apart (`a`, then `a_IConductor`); only a repeat of the same type takes `_2`. Quoted SysML names are not used, because every Workbench reader would need to change. Definitions keep the full EA title as their name. (D-0041, D-0042; amends IMP-095, IMP-112) | [REQ-31218](72_Derived_Requirements/IMP/REQ-31201_31220/REQ-31218%20-%20IMP-124%20Statement%204.md) — **Draft requirement:** "Two usages with one name take the name of their type to tell them apart (`a`, then `a_IConductor`); only a repeat of the same type takes `_2`." | Function: `BEH-00002`, `BEH-00004`, `BEH-00003`; Use case: `UC-00001`, `UC-00003`, `UC-00005`, `UC-00002`; Object: `OBJ-00001`, `OBJ-00002` | No direct rule-to-need link in registers | Active; direct; rule-derived link is provenance, other links provisional |

| [IMP-125](70_Rule_References/IMP/IMP-121_140/IMP-125.md) — Active · P1 — The importer can check a run before it writes: the full translation runs in memory and lists every flag, every deferred row and every problem, with a verdict. BLOCKED: the translation failed, a BOM error, or a file name the browser or Windows refuses. REVIEW: rows that would be skipped, conflicts with edits in the vault, BOM parts that differ from the vault, more than 10% of elements deferred, or over 50 BOM warnings. FLAGS: only flags. CLEAN: none. The check also says what becomes of every source row: standard; merged; imported but a value did not follow the standard (counted by code; nothing lost, something guessed); not in the model and may be lost (deferred or skipped by an error); text only; excluded by policy. It names the EA tables that are never read. (D-0041) | [REQ-31221](72_Derived_Requirements/IMP/REQ-31221_31240/REQ-31221%20-%20IMP-125%20Statement%201.md) — **Draft requirement:** "The importer can check a run before it writes: the full translation runs in memory and lists every flag, every deferred row and every problem, with a verdict." | Function: `BEH-00002`, `BEH-00007`, `BEH-00004`, `BEH-00003`; Use case: `UC-00001`, `UC-00008`, `UC-00009`, `UC-00003`, `UC-00005`, `UC-00002`; Object: `OBJ-00001`, `OBJ-00002` | No direct rule-to-need link in registers | Active; direct; rule-derived link is provenance, other links provisional |

| [IMP-126](70_Rule_References/IMP/IMP-121_140/IMP-126.md) — Active · P1 — An import into an existing vault shows the MDSE release, schema and every shipped plugin in the vault against the ones in the importer, with a state for each (same, newer in the importer, vault is newer, not installed, same version with different files). Updating the toolset during the import is a choice: ticked by default only when the importer is newer, unticked when the vault is newer, forced on for a new vault. Unticked, the model is imported and the installed tools stay as they are. Ticked, it applies the same update as the maintenance section, with replaced files saved first. (D-0041; D-0024) | [REQ-31227](72_Derived_Requirements/IMP/REQ-31221_31240/REQ-31227%20-%20IMP-126%20Statement%201.md) — **Draft requirement:** "An import into an existing vault shows the MDSE release, schema and every shipped plugin in the vault against the ones in the importer, with a state for each (same, newer in the importer, vault is newer, not installed, same version with different files)." | Function: `BEH-00002`, `BEH-00004`; Use case: `UC-00001`, `UC-00003`, `UC-00005`; Object: `OBJ-00001`, `OBJ-00002` | No direct rule-to-need link in registers | Active; direct; rule-derived link is provenance, other links provisional |

| [BOM-001](70_Rule_References/BOM/BOM-001_020/BOM-001.md) — Active · P2 — One reusable note per internal part number; revisions and occurrences never duplicate it. The parent assembly note owns its BOM usages with counts, quantities and applicability; a child note holds no data about the assemblies above it, and where-used is a reverse query, never written into the child. Separate discrete multiplicity from measured quantity and unit; preserve raw values; never guess units or double count. (REG-311, V2-22 (BOM Integration), D-0063) *Note: Owner-approved 2026-10-24 after removing the parent references.* | **No derived requirement or verification definition** | Function: `BEH-00002`, `BEH-00004`, `BEH-00007`; Use case: `UC-00001`, `UC-00005`, `UC-00008`; Object: `OBJ-00001` | No direct rule-to-need link in registers | Unreviewed; direct; rule-derived link is provenance, other links provisional |
| [BOM-002](70_Rule_References/BOM/BOM-001_020/BOM-002.md) — Unreviewed · P2 — One effective BOM per parent and configuration/revision. Competing simultaneous effective BOMs block automatic interpretation. Historical revisions stay in the source ledger. Family similarity is not automatically specialization, variation or substitutability. | [BOM-015](70_Rule_References/BOM/BOM-001_020/BOM-015.md) — Active · P2 — The importer reads the BOM from `.xlsx`, `.csv` or `.tsv` in the page. Two layouts are recognised by header names: flat (Parent Part Number, Child Part Number, Linenumber, Amount, Unit …) and redline (Number, CL1, CL2, level columns 0..7 or Structure). Part numbers are read as text. In a redline, unstruck text in a cell is current and struck text is the previous value; a part number struck with no replacement is a removed line. A sheet with no recognised header is refused with the reason. The redline layout was checked against the four real redlines (D-0040): the header may sit below a preamble, the level may be an unlabelled column just left of the part number, the number column may be `Part Number`, and the unit may be `Unit`, `UOM` or `Unit Of Measure` (when `Unit Of Measure` exists, `Unit` is the quantity). (D-0032, D-0040) | [REQ-31245](72_Derived_Requirements/BOM/REQ-31241_31260/REQ-31245%20-%20BOM-015%20Statement%201.md) — **Draft requirement:** "The importer reads the BOM from `.xlsx`, `.csv` or `.tsv` in the page." | Function: `BEH-00002`, `BEH-00004`; Use case: `UC-00001`, `UC-00005`; Object: `OBJ-00001` | No direct rule-to-need link in registers | Active; direct; rule-derived link is provenance, other links provisional |

Part numbers are read as text. In a redline, unstruck text in a cell is current and struck text is the previous value; a part number struck with no replacement is a removed line. A sheet with no recognised header is refused with the reason. The redline layout was checked against the four real redlines (D-0040): the header may sit below a preamble, the level may be an unlabelled column just left of the part number, the number column may be `Part Number`, and the unit may be `Unit`, `UOM` or `Unit Of Measure` (when `Unit Of Measure` exists, `Unit` is the quantity). (D-0032, D-0040) | [REQ-31249](72_Derived_Requirements/BOM/REQ-31241_31260/REQ-31249%20-%20BOM-015%20Statement%205.md) — **Draft requirement:** "The redline layout was checked against the four real redlines" | Function: `BEH-00002`, `BEH-00004`; Use case: `UC-00001`, `UC-00005`; Object: `OBJ-00001` | No direct rule-to-need link in registers | Active; direct; rule-derived link is provenance, other links provisional |

| [BOM-016](70_Rule_References/BOM/BOM-001_020/BOM-016.md) — Active · P2 · live — Each part number gets one `Object` (subtype part) note named `&lt;PN&gt;` (the part number alone, so the file name and the part number are the same value; the H1 is the part number and a `- Name:` line in Source repeats the name) in the folder of BOM-041: `part def P_&lt;PN&gt;` with `partNumber` (always a string), `revision` (CL1+CL2 as exported) and `source` attributes. Lifecycle state is not written (BOM-021). The part name is the `description` YAML property. The parent's block owns one part usage per line: whole-number counts with unit ea/piece become multiplicity; any other quantity is `quantity` plus the standard unit (BOM-019); line, pinned `childRevision`, reference designator, effectivity dates, SSC and notes are attributes. Drawing lines (name starts DWG) become a `Document` note under `BOM/Drawings` (with `item def D_&lt;PN&gt;`) that the parent references with `ref item` (BOM-025); they are not part usages. Alternate-source rows attach to the line above and are written as BOM-028. (D-0032, D-0048) | [REQ-31250](72_Derived_Requirements/BOM/REQ-31241_31260/REQ-31250%20-%20BOM-016%20Statement%201.md) — **Draft requirement:** "Each part number gets one `Object` (subtype part) note named `&lt;PN&gt;` (the part number alone, so the file name and the part number are the same value; the name is the H1 and a `- Name:` line in Source) under `BOM/Parts`: `part def P_&lt;PN&gt;` with `partNumber`, `revision` (CL1+CL2 as exported), `source`, `lifecycleState` attributes." | Function: `BEH-00002`, `BEH-00004`, `BEH-00007`; Use case: `UC-00001`, `UC-00005`, `UC-00008`; Object: `OBJ-00001` | No direct rule-to-need link in registers | Active; direct; rule-derived link is provenance, other links provisional |

| [BOM-017](70_Rule_References/BOM/BOM-001_020/BOM-017.md) — Active · P2 · live — The latest revision of a part number is the highest of the revisions it has as an assembly, or of those its parents pin when it is only a child (BOM-018); the part note lists the other revisions in the BOM. A parent pinning an older child revision gets a `flagConflict`; the child's body lists its older-revision users. Part numbers already noted in the vault are left as they are. Findings go to `99_System/11_Import/BOM Findings &lt;date&gt;.md`. EA to BOM matching by part number appearing in one EA element name, EA alias or PN tag is a report only. A part number that is already in the vault is kept; if the new file disagrees with it on any line (pinned revision, count or quantity, or a line that is new) the finding `EXISTING_DIFFERS` names every line that differs and nothing is rewritten. (D-0032, D-0040) | [REQ-31255](72_Derived_Requirements/BOM/REQ-31241_31260/REQ-31255%20-%20BOM-017%20Statement%201.md) — **Draft requirement:** "The latest revision of a part number is the one it has as an assembly, or the one every parent pins when it is only a child." | Function: `BEH-00002`, `BEH-00004`, `BEH-00007`; Use case: `UC-00001`, `UC-00005`, `UC-00008`; Object: `OBJ-00001` | No direct rule-to-need link in registers | Active; direct; rule-derived link is provenance, other links provisional |

| [WKB-004](70_Rule_References/WKB/WKB-001_020/WKB-004.md) — Unreviewed · P1 — Workbench runtime staging: bounded parallel shard reads; core model usable before Local Model hydration; heavy work staggered into non-overlapping lanes; elapsed-time work slices (12 ms) not fixed counts; background hydration is activity-aware, preemptible and promotable on demand; subsystems fail independently with separate health evidence; time-to-core-ready measured separately; cache persistence is single-flight and never starts hydration; views declare needsLocalOccurrences; no model I/O in onload or layout-ready. (REG-255, W-353b,W-354b,W-355b,W-356b,W-357,W-358,W-359,W-360,W-362,W-364b,W-365,W-367,W-369) | **No derived requirement or verification definition** | Function: `BEH-00003`, `BEH-00005`; Use case: `UC-00002`, `UC-00010`; Object: `OBJ-00003`, `OBJ-00002` | No direct rule-to-need link in registers | Unreviewed; direct; rule-derived link is provenance, other links provisional |
| [WKB-005](70_Rule_References/WKB/WKB-001_020/WKB-005.md) — Unreviewed · P3 — Workbench is an editor, not only a viewer, for Local Model context and reusable definitions through separate surfaces. | [WKB-006](70_Rule_References/WKB/WKB-001_020/WKB-006.md) — Unreviewed · P3 — Workbench has distinct structural and visual representations. A child usage is edited by locating its owner and changing the owning source region. | [WKB-007](70_Rule_References/WKB/WKB-001_020/WKB-007.md) — Unreviewed · P3 — Graph staleness: edits are validated at commit. | [WKB-012](70_Rule_References/WKB/WKB-001_020/WKB-012.md) — Active · P1 — Relationship properties are written inside the relationship in SysML: a `doc` comment, `attribute name = value;` (or `attribute name { doc … }` when the value is not a plain number or simple text), usage modifiers `ordered` and `nonunique`, quantity and direction. The drop-down offers only the options that relationship has; a free attribute name is accepted only where the relationship allows it (not specializes or verifies). Renaming a usage is refused when its name is used elsewhere in the block. | [WKB-014](70_Rule_References/WKB/WKB-001_020/WKB-014.md) — Active · P1 — The Edit lens has a Ports tab for a part, item, port or interface definition. | [WKB-015](70_Rule_References/WKB/WKB-001_020/WKB-015.md) — Active · P1 — The Ports tab checks before it previews: names are unique in the owner, a port or end is typed by a port definition, only items and attributes have a direction, a path must resolve through the owner's usages (inherited ones included), an interface connection joins ports, each port matches its end's type and conjugation, and a flow starts at an out (or inout) feature and ends at an in (or inout) one, with every `~` on the path reversing directions. Type or end mismatches that SysML allows (bound features of different types, both ports unconjugated) are shown as notes, not refusals. A member that has a body (properties, description) is not rewritten by the Ports tab. | [WKB-017](70_Rule_References/WKB/WKB-001_020/WKB-017.md) — Active · P1 — The Edit lens Merge tab marks aliases and merges them in two steps. | [WKB-019](70_Rule_References/WKB/WKB-001_020/WKB-019.md) — Active · P1 — A merge rewrites every other note: `[[Removed]]` becomes `[[Kept&#124;Removed]]` in text (frontmatter lists get the plain new name, without repeats or links to itself) and the removed definition's symbol becomes the kept one in every SysML block. A chain of aliases resolves to its end; many aliases merge in one preview, with the changed files counted, the removed files listed and one Undo that brings them all back. A note with more than one definition, a survivor with no SysML block and notes of different classes are refused with the reason. | [WKB-022](70_Rule_References/WKB/WKB-021_040/WKB-022.md) — Active · P1 — The Workbench keeps structure, design types, internal structure, function, states and requirements apart. Structure shows parts only. Design types shows only what a definition specializes and what specializes it. Internal shows the parts with the ports each part's type offers (inherited ones included), the definition's own ports, and every connection, binding and flow with both ends resolved; an end that names a part or port that does not exist is marked. Function shows actions and States shows states, each drawn alone. Requirements shows the trace around the selected note. The Obsidian panel has the same lenses. ## Aggregate inventory

- Rules: **386**
- Original requirement candidate notes: **332**
- Additional WKB-008 atomic requirement definitions: **9**
- Verification-case definitions: **159** (no execution established by the registers)
- Rules without a derived requirement or verification definition: **227**
- Total matrix rows: **727**
- Source IDs absent from current ruleset but recovered verbatim from the historical rule-reference notes: **9** — GOV-006, GOV-018, MOD-100, MOD-109, BOM-002, BOM-004, BOM-005, BOM-011, BOM-013. These may be retired/superseded; do not treat them as current requirements without disposition.

## Owner decisions and next validation

- **GOV-001:** Owner decided the trace should include **Manage Software Change** (function), **Integrate an Approved Software Change** (use case), and **Adaptable Tool** (stakeholder/customer need). This direction is recorded, but separate native model definitions and approved SysML edges have not yet been authored.
- **GOV-002:** still awaiting disposition. Technology and user dependency requirements are more accurately design/deployment constraints than a proven import or maintenance functional trace.
- Existing derived requirements and verifications remain **Draft** unless their own notes show a later decision. Do not mechanically turn these IDs or administrative source links into `#refinement`, `allocate`, or `verify` without an eligible native semantic owner.
- Review one rule at a time using this table, record owner dispositions, update native model definitions where justified, and validate parser/graph/viewpoint synchronization.
